Given positive numbers

(\mathsf{c}_{1},\mathsf{c}_{2},\mathsf{c}_{3})
, we say that
\mathfrak{X}
satisfies
\ATP_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\mathsf{c}_{1};\mathsf{c}_{2};\mathsf{c}_{3})
if for all

[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\in\mathds{R}^{p}
,

(172)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W})\|_{2}^{2}\geq\bigg[\mathsf{c}_{1}\|[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}-\mathsf{c}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})-\mathsf{c}_{3}\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{W})\bigg]_{+}^{2}-2|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}|.

In addition, given positive numbers

(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{3})
, we will say that
(\mathfrak{X},\boldsymbol{\xi})
satisfies
\MP_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\mathsf{f}_{1};\mathsf{f}_{2};\mathsf{f}_{3})
if for all
[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\in\mathds{R}^{p}
,

(173)

\displaystyle|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W})\rangle|\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}+\mathsf{f}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\mathsf{f}_{3}\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{W}).

The next lemma states that
\ATP
is a consequence of

\TP
and
\PP
. We omit the proof as it follows similar reasoning of Lemma
8.2
.

Lemma 9.3

(
\TP+\PP\Rightarrow\ATP
)
.

Let positive numbers
\mathsf{a}_{1},\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1}
,
\mathsf{a}_{2}
,
\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}
,
\mathsf{b}_{1}
,
\mathsf{b}_{2}
and
\mathsf{b}_{3}
with
\mathsf{b}_{1}<\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1}
. Suppose that
\mathfrak{X}
satisfies:

(i)

\TP_{\|\cdot\|_{\Pi},\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{a}_{1};\mathsf{a}_{2})
.

(ii)

\TP_{\|\cdot\|,\mathcal{Q}}(\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1};\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2})
.

(iii)

\PP_{\|\cdot\|_{\Pi},\mathcal{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\mathsf{b}_{1};\mathsf{b}_{2};\mathsf{b}_{3};\beta\mathsf{b}_{2}\mathsf{b}_{3})
for some
\beta\in(0,1]
.

Then, for any
\alpha>0
such that
\alpha^{2}+\mathsf{b}_{1}\leq(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})^{2}
,

\mathfrak{X}
satisfies the
\ATP_{\|\cdot\|_{\Pi},\mathcal{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\mathsf{c}_{1};\mathsf{c}_{2};\mathsf{c}_{3})
in Definition
9.2
with constants

\mathsf{c}_{1}=\sqrt{(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})^{2}-\mathsf{b}_{1}-\alpha^{2}}
,
\mathsf{c}_{2}=\mathsf{a}_{2}+\mathsf{b}_{2}/\alpha
,
\mathsf{c}_{3}=\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}+\mathsf{b}_{3}/\alpha
. Taking
\alpha=(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})/2
, we obtain that

\ATP(\mathsf{c}_{1};\mathsf{c}_{2};\mathsf{c}_{3})
holds with constants

\mathsf{c}_{1}=\sqrt{(3/4)(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})^{2}-\mathsf{b}_{1}}
,
\mathsf{c}_{2}=\mathsf{a}_{2}+2\mathsf{b}_{2}/(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})
and

\mathsf{c}_{3}=\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}+2\mathsf{b}_{3}/(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})
.

We will need an additional cone definition.

Definition 9.4
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{Q}
be decomposable norms on
\mathds{R}^{p}
(see Definition
8.3
in Section
8.1
). Given
[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]\in(\mathds{R}^{p})^{2}
, let

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}
denote the projection maps associated to
(\mathcal{R},\mathbf{B})
and
(\mathcal{Q},\boldsymbol{\Gamma})
respectively. Given
c_{0},\gamma>0
, we define the cone
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma},\mathcal{R},\mathcal{Q}}(c_{0},\gamma)
by

(174)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle:=\left\{[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]:\gamma\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\perp}(\mathbf{V}))+\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\perp}(\mathbf{W}))\leq c_{0}\left[\gamma\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}))+\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\mathbf{W}))\right]\right\}.

We will omit the subscripts
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{Q}
when the norms are clear in the context.

9.2
Deterministic bounds

Throughout this section, we work with the nuclear norm

\|\cdot\|_{N}
and the
\ell_{1}
-norm
\|\cdot\|_{1}
on
\mathds{R}^{p}
. With some abuse of notation,
we will denote by
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}
the projection associated to
(\|\cdot\|_{N},\mathbf{B}^{*})
and by

\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}
the projection associated to
(\|\cdot\|_{1},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*})
(see Definition
8.3
in Section
8.1
).

Lemma 9.5

(Dimension reduction)
.

Grant Assumption
2
and suppose that:

(i)

(\mathfrak{X},\boldsymbol{\xi})
satisfies the
\MP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathsf{f}_{1};\mathsf{f}_{2};\mathsf{f}_{3})

for some positive numbers
\mathsf{f}_{1}
,
\mathsf{f}_{2}
and
\mathsf{f}_{3}
.

(ii)

\mathfrak{X}
satisfies the
\ATP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathsf{c}_{1};\mathsf{c}_{2};\mathsf{c}_{3})
for some positive numbers
\mathsf{c}_{1},\mathsf{c}_{2},\mathsf{c}_{3}
.

(iii)

\lambda=\gamma\tau\geq 4(\mathsf{f}_{2}\vee\mathsf{c}_{2}),\quad\text{and}\quad\tau\geq 4[\mathsf{c}_{3}\vee(\mathsf{f}_{3}+\nicefrac{{4\mathsf{a}^{*}}}{{\sqrt{n}}})].

(iv)

2\mathsf{f}_{1}\leq\mathsf{c}_{1}
.

Define

(175)

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle:=(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}-(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}+(\nicefrac{{3\tau}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}-(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}.

Then either
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(3,\gamma)
and

(176)

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{c}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}-(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N})-(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}\right)_{+}^{2}\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}+\triangle.

or
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(6,\gamma)
and

(177)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}{4}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}+\triangle.

or

(178)

\displaystyle\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq 80\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{4}},

(179)

\displaystyle\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 37.4\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}.

Proof.

By the first order condition of (
2.2
) at
[\hat{\mathbf{B}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]
, there exist

\mathbf{V}\in\partial\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{N}
and
\mathbf{W}\in\partial\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}
such that for all
[\mathbf{B};\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]
satisfying
\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}
,

(180)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[y_{i}^{(n)}-\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}_{i}(\hat{\mathbf{B}}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\right]\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}^{(n)}_{i},\hat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\hat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle,

(181)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[y_{i}^{(n)}-\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}_{i}(\hat{\mathbf{B}}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\right]\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}^{(n)}_{i},\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle.

Evaluating at
[\mathbf{B}^{*};\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}]
, which satisfies

\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}
by assumption, and using (
10
)
we get

(182)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle-\lambda\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle-\tau\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle.

Using
\MP
in item (i) and

(183)

\displaystyle-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\leq\|\mathbf{B}\|_{N}-\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{N},\quad\quad-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\|_{1}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1},

we get

(184)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}+\mathsf{f}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\mathsf{f}_{3}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}

(185)

\displaystyle+\lambda\big(\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{N}-\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{N}\big)+\tau\big(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}\big).

For convenience, we define the quantity

(186)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}:=(\mathsf{c}_{2}\vee\mathsf{f}_{2})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\left[\mathsf{c}_{3}\vee\left(\mathsf{f}_{3}+\frac{4\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1},

By norm duality and isotropy,
|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}|=|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle|\leq\frac{2\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}
. This fact, the previous display and

\ATP
in item (ii) imply

(187)

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{c}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}-\widetilde{H}\right)_{+}^{2}\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}+\widetilde{H}+\lambda\big(\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{N}-\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{N}\big)+\tau\big(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}\big).

We now divide in two cases.

Case 1:

\widetilde{H}\geq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}
.

We obtain that the RHS of (
187
) is upper bounded by

(188)

\displaystyle 2(\mathsf{c}_{2}\vee\mathsf{f}_{2})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+2\left[\mathsf{c}_{3}\vee\left(\mathsf{f}_{3}+\frac{4\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}+\lambda\big(\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{N}-\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{N}\big)+\tau\big(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}\big)

(189)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm(iii)}}{{\leq}}(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}+\lambda\big(\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{N}-\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{N}\big)+\tau\big(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}\big)

(190)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm Lemma\mbox{ }\ref{lemma:A1:B}}}{{\leq}}(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}-(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}+(\nicefrac{{3\tau}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}-(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1},

implying
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(3,\gamma)
. The above bound also implies, again by (iii) and (
187
), inequality (
176
).

Case 2:

\widetilde{H}\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}
.

A similar bound of Case 1, using (iii) and Lemma
8.4
with
\nu=1/4
, implies that
\widetilde{H}+\lambda\big(\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{N}-\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{N}\big)+\tau\big(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}\big)
is upper bounded by

(191)

\displaystyle(\nicefrac{{5\lambda}}{{4}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}-(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{4}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}+(\nicefrac{{5\tau}}{{4}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}-(\nicefrac{{3\tau}}{{4}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}\leq\triangle.

This fact,
2\mathsf{f}_{1}\leq\mathsf{c}_{1}
in item (iv) and (
187
) imply

(192)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}{4}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}+\triangle.

Define
H:=(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}+(\nicefrac{{3\tau}}{{2}})\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}

We further consider two subcases.

Case 2.1:

\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}\leq H
.

In that case, we conclude from (
192
) that

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(6,\gamma)
.

Case 2.2:

\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}\geq H
.

Let
G:=\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}
. In that case we obtain
\frac{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}{4}G^{2}\leq 2\mathsf{f}_{1}G\Rightarrow G\leq\frac{8\mathsf{f}_{1}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}
. Therefore
H\leq 8\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}
. From (
192
), we obtain that

(193)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}+\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}\leq 4\mathsf{f}_{1}G\leq 32\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}},

which further implies

(194)

\displaystyle\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{2}{3}H+32\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\leq 37.4\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}.

Finally, from (
192
) and
H\leq 8\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}
,

(195)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}{4}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}+8\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}},

which implies (
178
) by Young’s inequality.

∎

Theorem 9.6

(Trace-regression with matrix decomposition)
.

Suppose that, in addition to (i)-(iv) in Lemma
9.5
, the following condition holds:

(v)

If we define

(196)

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle:=\Psi_{\|\cdot\|_{N}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}))\cdot\mu(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(12)),

(197)

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle:=\Psi_{\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}))\cdot\mu(\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(12)),

assume that

\frac{20}{3}\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}}\leq\mathsf{c}_{1}.

Then

(198)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq 80\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{4}}\bigvee\left[\frac{4\mathsf{f}_{1}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{28}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}}\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{4\mathsf{f}_{1}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{40}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}(\lambda R)\vee(\tau Q)\right],

(199)

\displaystyle\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 37.4\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\bigvee\left[\frac{14\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{32}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{10\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{270}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}(\lambda^{2}R^{2})\vee(\tau^{2}Q^{2})\right].

Proof.

In Lemma
9.5
, if (
178
)-(
179
) hold there is nothing to prove. We thus need to consider the case (A) for which

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(3,\gamma)
and (
176
) hold or case (B) for which

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(6,\gamma)
and (
177
) hold.

Case (A)
. We first claim that, since
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(3,\gamma)
, we may assume that either
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(12)
or
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(12)
. Indeed, otherwise,

9\gamma\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}+9\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}\leq 0

implying
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}=0
.

Case 1:

\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(12)
and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(12)
. Decomposability,

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(3,\gamma)
and Cauchy-Schwarz imply

(200)

\displaystyle\frac{\triangle}{3}\leq(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\|_{\Pi}.

Assuming
5\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}}\leq\mathsf{c}_{1}
, a similar argument in Proposition
2
implies that

(201)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{6}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}},

(202)

\displaystyle\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 5\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{20}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}(\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}).

Case 2:

\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\notin\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(12)
and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(12)
. As

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(3,\gamma)
,

(203)

\displaystyle 9\gamma\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})\|_{N}+\|\mathcal{P}^{\perp}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1}\leq 3\|\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\|_{1},

implying

(204)

\displaystyle\frac{\triangle}{3}\leq(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}\leq 20\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{10}{3}\tau Q\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{\Pi}.

Assuming
\frac{20}{3}\tau Q\leq\mathsf{c}_{1}
, we obtain

(205)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{40}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\tau Q,

(206)

\displaystyle\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{3\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{270}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\tau^{2}Q^{2}.

Case 3:

\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(12)
and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\notin\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(12)
. Similarly to Case 2,
assuming
\frac{20}{3}\lambda R\leq\mathsf{c}_{1}
, we obtain

(207)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{40}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\lambda R,

(208)

\displaystyle\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{3\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{270}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\lambda^{2}R^{2}.

Case (B).
Without further conditions,
\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}\leq\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}(\mathsf{f}_{1}+\triangle)
. By dividing in subcases as in Case (A), one obtains the bounds

(209)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}};\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{28}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2}}\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{12}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\tau Q\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{12}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\lambda R\right],

(210)

\displaystyle\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}}\|_{N}+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left[14\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{32}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}(\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\tau^{2}Q^{2})\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{16}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\tau^{2}Q^{2}\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\mathsf{f}_{1}+\frac{16}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right].

The proof is finished.
∎

9.3
Properties for subgaussian
(\mathbf{X},\xi)

Throughout this section, we additionally assume
(\mathbf{X},\xi)\in\mathds{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}
is a centered (not necessarily independent) random pair satisfying Assumption
3
and
\{(\mathbf{X}_{i},\xi_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
is an iid copy of
(\mathbf{X},\xi)
. Moreover,
\mathcal{R}
is any norm on
\mathds{R}^{p}
and
\mathcal{Q}
is any norm on

\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

In this section we prove that all properties of Definitions
9.1
and
9.2
are satisfied with high-probability. Recall that
\TP
in Definition
8.1
was already proved in Proposition
3
in Section
8.3
.

We first prove
\PP
. We will use the next lemma which is immediate from Theorem
11.8
in the Appendix for the product process (
169
) over the linear class
\mathcal{F}=\{\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\cdot,\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle:\mathbf{V}\in\mathds{R}^{p}\}
.

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{2}
be bounded subsets of

\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}
. There exists universal numerical constant
C>0
, such that, for any
n\geq 1
and
t>0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

(211)

\displaystyle\sup_{[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\in\mathcal{B}_{1}\times\mathcal{B}_{2}}\left|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{n}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathcal{B}_{1}))\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathcal{B}_{2}))

(212)

\displaystyle+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathcal{B}_{1})+\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathcal{B}_{2})\right]

(213)

\displaystyle+CL^{2}\left(\frac{t}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\right).

Proposition 6

(
\PP
)
.

There exists universal numerical constant
C>0
, such that for all
\delta\in(0,1]

and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with probability at least

1-\delta
, the following property holds: for all

[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\in(\mathds{R}^{p})^{2}
,

(214)

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{n}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}\right|

\displaystyle\leq CL^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\right)\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}

(215)

\displaystyle+CL\frac{\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}

(216)

\displaystyle+CL\frac{\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{W})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}})\cap\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}\big\|\mathbf{V}\big\|_{\Pi}

(217)

\displaystyle+C\frac{\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{W})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}})\cap\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}.

Proof.

Let
R_{1},R_{2}>0
and define the sets

(218)

\displaystyle V_{1}:=\{\mathbf{V}:\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\leq 1,\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\leq R_{1}\},\quad V_{2}:=\{\mathbf{W}:\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}\leq 1,\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{W})\leq R_{2}\}.

Note that,

(219)

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{1}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap R_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right),\quad\quad\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{2}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}})\cap R_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right).

Define for convenience the functions

(220)

\displaystyle g(r):=\frac{\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap r^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}r,\quad\bar{g}(\bar{r}):=\frac{\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}})\cap{\bar{r}}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}\bar{r}.

By Lemma
9.7
, there is universal constant
C>0
such that, for any
R_{1},R_{2}>0
and
\delta\in(0,1]
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

(221)

\displaystyle\sup_{[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\in V_{1}\times V_{2}}|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{n}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}|

\displaystyle\leq Cg(R_{1})\bar{g}(R_{2})+CL[g(R_{1})+\bar{g}(R_{2})]

(222)

\displaystyle+CL^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\right).

We can now use a bi-parameter peeling lemma for subexponential tails (analogously to Lemma
11.2
for subgaussian tails) with set
V:=\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}
, functions

M(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}):=-|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{n}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}|
,

h_{1}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}):=\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})
,
h_{2}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}):=\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{W})
,
g
and
\bar{g}
and constants
c:=1
and
b_{1}:=CL^{2}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n})
. The claim follows from such lemma and the homogeneity of norms.
∎

The next proposition follows from the concentration bound for the multiplier process (Theorem
11.6
in the Appendix) and a peeling lemma. The proof is similar to Proposition
5
so we omit the details.

Proposition 7

(
\MP
)
.

For
t,s>0
, let
\triangle(t,s)
as defined in Proposition
5
. There exists universal constant
C>0
,
c_{0},c\geq 2
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\delta\in(0,1/c]
and
\rho\in(0,1/c_{0}]
, with probability at least

1-\delta-\rho
, the following property holds: for all

[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\in(\mathds{R}^{p})^{2}
,

(223)

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma L\cdot\triangle(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\delta}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}})\cdot\|[\mathbf{V};\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}

(224)

\displaystyle+C\sigma L\left[1+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\rho)}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]\frac{\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}}))}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})

(225)

\displaystyle+C\sigma L\left[1+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\rho)}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]\frac{\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}}))}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{W}).

9.4
Proof of Theorem
4.3

We will apply Sections
8.3
and
9.3
to
the nuclear and
\ell_{1}
norms in
\mathds{R}^{p}
. By Assumption
2
,
\mathfrak{S}
is the identity operator. As before,

\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}_{\|\cdot\|_{N}})\lesssim\sqrt{d_{1}}+\sqrt{d_{2}}

and

\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}_{\|\cdot\|_{1}})\lesssim\sqrt{\log p}
.

We first use Proposition
3
with
\mathcal{R}=\|\cdot\|_{N}
,

\epsilon=\frac{c}{C^{2}L^{2}}
for sufficiently small
c\in(0,1)
and assuming

(226)

\displaystyle\delta\geq\exp\left(-c_{1}\frac{n}{L^{4}}\right)

for large enough constant
c_{1}>0
. It follows that for

\mathsf{a}_{1}\in(0,1)
an universal constant and

\mathsf{a}_{2}\asymp L\left(\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{d_{2}}{n}}\right),

on an event
\Omega_{1}
of probability at least
1-\delta/4
, property

\TP_{\|\cdot\|_{N}}(\mathsf{a}_{1};\mathsf{a}_{2})
is satisfied. Similarly, assuming (
226
), we have that for universal constant
\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1}\in(0,1)
and

\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}\asymp L\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}},

on an event
\Omega_{1}^{\prime}
of probability at least
1-\delta/4
, property

\TP_{\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1};\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2})
is satisfied.

From Proposition
6
, for every
\delta\in(0,1)
and taking

\mathsf{b}_{1}\asymp L^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\right),\quad\mathsf{b}_{2}\asymp L\left(\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{d_{2}}{n}}\right),\quad\mathsf{b}_{3}\asymp L\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}

and
\mathsf{b}_{4}=\frac{\mathsf{b}_{2}\mathsf{b}_{3}}{L^{2}}
(recall
L\geq 1
), we have that on an event
\Omega_{2}
of probability at least
1-\delta/4
, property
\PP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathsf{b}_{1};\mathsf{b}_{2};\mathsf{b}_{3};\mathsf{b}_{4})
is satisfied.

From Lemma
8.2
, by enlarging
c_{1}
in (
226
) if necessary, for
\mathsf{c}_{1}\in(0,1)
an universal constant and

\mathsf{c}_{2}\asymp L\left(\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{d_{2}}{n}}\right),\quad\mathsf{c}_{3}\asymp L\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}},

\ATP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathsf{c}_{1};\mathsf{c}_{2};\mathsf{c}_{3})
is satisfied on
\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{2}
.

We now use Proposition
7
(with

\delta=\rho
). By enlarging
c_{1}
in (
226
) if necessary, if we take

\mathsf{f}_{1}\asymp\sigma L\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\mathsf{f}_{2}\asymp\sigma L\left(\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{d_{2}}{n}}\right),\quad\mathsf{f}_{3}\asymp\sigma L\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}},

we have by Proposition
7
that on an event
\Omega_{3}
of probability at least
1-\delta/4
,

\MP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathsf{f}_{1};\mathsf{f}_{2};\mathsf{f}_{3})
is satisfied.

By an union bound and enlarging constants, for
\delta
satisfying (
226
), on the event

\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega_{3}
of probability at least
1-\delta
, all properties
\TP
,
\PP
,
\ATP
and

\MP
hold with constants as specified above. We assume such event is realized and invoke Theorem
9.6
. It is straightforward to check

\rm{(iii)}
by the definitions of
\tau
and
\lambda
in Theorem
4.3
. Item
\rm{(iv)}
is tantamount requiring
\delta\geq\exp(-c_{2}n/\sigma^{2}L^{2})
for universal constant
c_{2}>0
.
We now check item
\rm{(v)}
. In our setting,
R\leq\sqrt{r}\mu(\mathbf{B}^{*})
with
\mu(\mathbf{B}^{*}):=\mu(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(12))
and
Q\leq\sqrt{s}\mu(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*})
with
\mu(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}):=\mu(\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(12))
. Condition in item
\rm{(iv)}
requires

(227)

\displaystyle C\sigma^{2}L^{2}\left(\frac{d_{1}}{n}+\frac{d_{2}}{n}\right)r\mu^{2}(\mathbf{B}^{*})+C\left(\sigma^{2}L^{2}\frac{\log p}{n}+\frac{(\mathsf{a}^{*})^{2}}{n}\right)s\mu^{2}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*})<1.

As the feature is isotropic,
\mu(\mathbf{B}^{*})=\mu(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*})=1
. Thus the above condition holds by assumption. With all conditions of Theorem
9.6
taking place, the rate in Theorem
4.3
follows.

9.5
Proof of Proposition
1

In trace-regression with matrix decomposition, the design is random. Up to conditioning on the feature data, the proof of Proposition
1
follows almost identical arguments in the proof of Theorem 2 in
[
2
]
for the matrix decomposition problem (
8
) with identity design
(\mathfrak{X}=I)
. We present a sketch here for completeness.

We prepare the ground so to apply Fano’s method. For any

\boldsymbol{\Theta}:=[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]\in(\mathds{R}^{p})^{2}
, we define for convenience

\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\|_{F}^{2}:=\|\mathbf{B}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\|_{F}^{2}.

Given
\eta>0
and
M\in\mathbb{N}
, a
\eta
-packing of
\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
of size
M
is a finite subset

\mathcal{A}=\{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{M}\}
of

\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
satisfying

\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\ell}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\|_{F}\geq\eta

for all
\ell\neq k
. For model (
10
), let
y_{1}^{n}:=\{y_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
and
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}:=\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
.
For any
k\in[M]
and
i\in[n]
, we will denote by

P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}

(or
P^{k}_{y_{i}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}
)
the conditional distribution of
y_{1}^{n}
(or
y_{i}
) given

\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}
corresponding to the model (
10
) with parameters
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}=[\mathbf{B}_{k},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}]
belonging to the packing
\mathcal{A}
. Being normal distributions, they are mutually absolute continuous. We denote by
\mathsf{KL}(\mathbf{P}\|\mathbf{Q})
the Kullback-Leibler divergence between probability measures
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
. In that setting, Fano’s method assures that

(228)

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}\in\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})}\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}}\left\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}\geq\eta^{2}\right\}\geq 1-\frac{\frac{1}{\binom{M}{2}}\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\right)+\log 2}{\log M}.

The proof follows from an union bound on the following two separate lower bounds.

Lower bound on the low-spikeness bias
. It is sufficient to give a lower bound on
\mathcal{A}(1,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
. We define the subset
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{A}(1,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
with size
M=4
by

(229)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}:=\{[\mathbf{B}^{*},-\mathbf{B}^{*}],[-\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathbf{B}^{*}],(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})[\mathbf{B}^{*},-\mathbf{B}^{*}],[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}]\}

using the matrix
\mathbf{B}^{*}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
defined by

\mathbf{B}^{*}:=\frac{\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}\right]\underbrace{\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}1&1&\cdots&1&0&\cdots&0\end{array}\right]^{\top}}_{\boldsymbol{f}^{\top}},

where
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
has
s
unit coordinates. It is easy to check that
\mathcal{A}
is a
\eta
-packing of

\mathcal{A}(1,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
with
\eta=c_{0}\mathsf{a}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}
for some constant
c_{0}>0
. For any element
[\mathbf{B}_{k},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}]
of
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathbf{B}_{k}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}=0
implying
P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}(0,n\sigma^{2}\mathbf{I})
. Hence, for any
k\neq\ell
,

\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\right)=0.

From (
228
), one obtains a lower bound with rate of order
\mathsf{a}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}
with positive probability.

Lower bound on the estimation error
. From the packing constructions in Lemmas 5 and 6 in
[
2
]
, one may show that, for
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 10
,
\mathsf{a}^{*}\geq 32\sqrt{\log p}
,
s<p
and any
\eta>0
, there exists a
\eta
-packing
\mathcal{A}=\{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\}_{k\in[M]}
of
\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
with size

(230)

\displaystyle M\geq\frac{1}{4}\exp\left\{\frac{s}{2}\log\frac{p-s}{s/2}+\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{256}\right\},

satisfying
\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\|_{F}\leq 3\eta
for any
k\in[M]
. By independence of
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
and
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}
and isotropy,

(231)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\right)=\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{i}|\mathbf{X}_{i}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{i}|\mathbf{X}_{i}}\right)=\frac{n\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\leq\frac{18n}{\sigma^{2}}\eta^{2}.

From (
228
) and (
230
), one then checks that tacking

(232)

\displaystyle\eta^{2}:=c_{o}\sigma^{2}\left\{\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}+\frac{s}{n}\log\left(\frac{p-s}{s/2}\right)\right\},

for some constant
c_{0}>0
, one obtains a lower bound with rate of order
\eta^{2}
with positive probability.

10
Proof of Theorem
4.4

Recall Definition
8.1
in Section
8.1
. We will work with a variation of
\MP
.

Definition 10.1

(
\MP
)
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a norm on
\mathds{R}^{p}
and
\mathcal{Q}
be a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Given non-negative numbers
(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{4})
, we say
(\mathfrak{X},\boldsymbol{\xi})
satisfies

\MP_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{4})
if

(233)

\displaystyle\forall[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathds{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\rangle|\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}+\mathsf{f}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\mathsf{f}_{4}\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u}).

We will also need additional cone definitions.

Definition 10.2
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a norm on
\mathds{R}^{p}
and
\mathcal{Q}
be a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Given

\mathsf{h}>0
, we define the cones

(234)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{h})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{V}:\mathsf{h}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\right\},

(235)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}(\mathsf{h})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{V}:\mathsf{h}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathbf{V})\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}\right\},

(236)

\displaystyle\mathds{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathsf{h})

\displaystyle:=\left\{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]:\mathsf{h}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u})\leq\|[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}\right\}.

The aim of this section is to prove that, with high probability,

\ATP
holds over an appropriate cone
\mathbb{C}
and
\MP_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)},\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}
holds everywhere. Throughout this section, we additionally assume
(\mathbf{X},\xi)\in\mathds{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}
satisfies Assumption
4
and
\{(\mathbf{X}_{i},\xi_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
is an iid copy of
(\mathbf{X},\xi)
. Given a compact set
V\subset\mathds{R}^{p}
, we shall need the Gaussian complexity

\mathcal{G}(V):=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{X}\left(V\right)\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathfrak{X}_{i}(\mathbf{V})\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}\Bigg|\mathfrak{X}\right]\right],

where
\boldsymbol{\xi}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{0},\mathbf{I}_{n})
is independent of
\mathbf{X}
. Define also

\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(V):=\sqrt{p}\mathcal{G}(V)
. We note that, by the dual-norm inequality, given
\mathbf{V}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
,

|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle|\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}.

In particular,
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}=\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}
.

The next result states a
lower bound
on the quadratic process. The main tool to prove this lemma is a
one-sided
version of a Bernstein’s inequality for bounded processes due to Bousquet
[
5
]
.

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\mathbb{S}_{\infty}^{p}:=\{\mathbf{V}\in\mathds{R}^{p}:\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}=1\}
and
V\subset R\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\cap\mathbb{S}_{\infty}^{p}
for some
R\in[0,1]
. Then, for any
n\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-t)
,

(237)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{2}\right]\leq 2\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{\mathcal{G}(V)}{n}+2R\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}+\frac{t}{3n}.

Proof.

Define

X_{i,\mathbf{V}}:=\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{2}.

By the symmetrization inequality (e.g. Exercise 11.5 in
[
5
]
), we have

(238)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i\in[n]}X_{i,\mathbf{V}}\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i\in[n]}\epsilon_{i}X_{i,\mathbf{V}}\right],

where
\{\epsilon_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
are iid Rademacher variables independent of
X:=\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. One may bound the Rademacher complexity by the Gaussian complexity as

(239)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i\in[n]}\epsilon_{i}X_{i,\mathbf{V}}\bigg|X\right]\leq\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i\in[n]}g_{i}X_{i,\mathbf{V}}\bigg|X\right],

where
\{g_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
is an iid
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
sequence independent of
\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
.

We will now use an standard argument via Slepian’s inequality over the randomness of

\{g_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
for the process
\mathbf{V}\mapsto Z_{\mathbf{V}}:=\sum_{i\in[n]}g_{i}X_{i,\mathbf{V}}
. One has

(240)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(Z_{\mathbf{V}}-Z_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}})^{2}|X]=\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{2}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{2}\leq 4\|\mathfrak{X}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{V}^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}.

Define the Gaussian process
W_{\mathbf{V}}:=2\langle\mathfrak{X}(\mathbf{V}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\rangle
with
\boldsymbol{\xi}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}(0,\mathbf{I}_{n})
independent of
\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. Under the above conditions, Slepian’s inequality implies

(241)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}Z_{\mathbf{V}}\bigg|X\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}W_{\mathbf{V}}\bigg|X\right]=2\mathscr{G}(\mathfrak{X}(V)).

Define
Z:=\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i\in[n]}X_{i,\mathbf{V}}
. The previous displays imply

\mathbb{E}Z\leq 2\sqrt{2\pi}\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}(\mathfrak{X}(V))]
.

We now establish concentration. For all
i\in[n]
and
\mathbf{V}\in V
,

X_{i,\mathbf{V}}\leq R^{2}\leq 1.

Since, for each
\mathbf{V}\in V
,
\{X_{i,\mathbf{V}}\}_{i\in[n]}
are independent and identically distributed, we may apply Bousquet’s
inequality (e.g. Corollary 12.2 in
[
5
]
). We thus get that, for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-t)
,

(242)

\displaystyle Z\leq\mathbb{E}Z+\sigma\sqrt{2t}+\frac{t}{3},

where
\sigma^{2}:=\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}[X_{i,\mathbf{V}}^{2}]
. Since for
\mathbf{V}\in V
,
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\vee\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, it is easy to check that

\mathbb{E}(\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{2}-\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2})^{2}=\mathbb{E}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{4}+\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{4}\leq 2R^{2}.

In particular,

(243)

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}=\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}[X_{i,\mathbf{V}}^{2}]\leq 2R^{2}n.

This finishes the proof.
∎

The next proposition is proved using the previous lemma and a peeling argument.

Proposition 8

(
\TP
)
.

There are universal constants
\mathsf{c}_{0},c_{0}\in(0,1)
for which the following holds. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
and define the number

(244)

\displaystyle\mathsf{h}_{\delta}:=\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})}{c_{0}n}\bigvee\frac{\sqrt{1+\log(1/\delta)}}{c_{0}\sqrt{n}},

and the cone

\mathbb{C}(\delta):=\mathfrak{C}\left(\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\right)\bigcap\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\right).

Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
the design operator
\mathfrak{X}
satisfies

(245)

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{C}(\delta)}\frac{\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}}\geq\mathsf{c}_{0}.

Proof.

Fix
\alpha>1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. We define the set
S(\delta):=\mathbb{C}(\delta)\cap\mathbb{S}_{\infty}
and, for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the set

(246)

\displaystyle S_{\ell}(\delta)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{S}_{\infty}^{p}:\alpha^{\ell-1}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}<\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta},\quad\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathbf{V})\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}\right\}.

Note that for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

S_{\ell}(\delta)\subset r\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}}
with
r:=\sqrt{p}\alpha^{2\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}
. Hence,

\mathcal{G}(S_{\ell}(\delta))=\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}(\mathfrak{X}(S_{\ell}(\delta)))]\leq\sqrt{p}\alpha^{2\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}}))]=\alpha^{2\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}}).

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
S_{\ell}(\delta)\subset\mathbb{S}_{\infty}^{p}\cap r_{\ell}\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}
with
r_{\ell}:=\min\{1,\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\}
. Hence, by Lemma
10.3
and the previous displayed bound, it holds that, for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
t>0
, on an event
\mathcal{E}_{\ell}(t)
of probability
\geq 1-e^{-t}
, for all
\mathbf{V}\in S_{\ell}(\delta)
,

(247)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}-\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{\alpha^{2\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})}{n}+2r_{\ell}\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}+\frac{t}{3n}.

Let
c\in(0,1)
absolute constant to be determined. By an union bound the event

\mathcal{E}:=\cap_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}\left(cn\alpha^{2\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}^{2}\right)

is such that

(248)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-cn\alpha^{2\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}^{2}\right)\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-2cn\log(\alpha)\mathsf{h}_{\delta}^{2}\cdot\ell\right)\leq\frac{\exp\left(-2cn\log(\alpha)\mathsf{h}_{\delta}^{2}\right)}{1-\exp\left(-2cn\log(\alpha)\mathsf{h}_{\delta}^{2}\right)}.

Next, we assume that

\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\geq\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})}{cn}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log(2)}{2c\log(\alpha)\cdot n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2c\log(\alpha)\cdot n}}.

In particular,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\leq\delta
and

\frac{\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})}{n}\leq c\mathsf{h}_{\delta}^{2}.

The rest of the proof will occur on the event
\mathcal{E}
. Let

\mathbf{V}\in S(\delta)
. There is
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that
\mathbf{V}\in S_{\ell}(\delta)
. Since
\mathbb{S}_{\infty}^{p}\subset\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}
, we have two cases:

Case 1:

\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}<1
. From (
248
),

(249)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}-\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2\pi}\cdot c(\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta})^{2}+2\sqrt{c}(\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta})^{2}+\frac{c}{3}(\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta})^{2}

(250)

\displaystyle\leq\left(2\sqrt{2\pi}\cdot c+2\sqrt{c}+\frac{c}{3}\right)\alpha^{2}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}.

Case 2:

\alpha^{\ell-1}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}<1\leq\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta}
. From (
248
),

(251)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}-\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2\pi}\cdot c(\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta})^{2}+2\sqrt{c}(\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta})+\frac{c}{3}(\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta})^{2}

(252)

\displaystyle\leq\left(2\sqrt{2\pi}\cdot c+2\sqrt{c}+\frac{c}{3}\right)(\alpha^{\ell}\mathsf{h}_{\delta})^{2}

(253)

\displaystyle\leq\left(2\sqrt{2\pi}\cdot c+2\sqrt{c}+\frac{c}{3}\right)\alpha^{2}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}.

Choose
c=c(\alpha)\in(0,1)
small enough so that

a:=((2\sqrt{2\pi}+3^{-1})c+2\sqrt{c})\alpha^{2}<1

and set
\mathsf{c}_{0}^{2}:=1-a
. We conclude that

\inf_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{C}(\delta)\cap\mathbb{S}_{\infty}^{p}}\frac{\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}}\geq\mathsf{c}_{0}.

The fact that

\inf_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{C}(\delta)}\frac{\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}}\geq\mathsf{c}_{0}

follows from homogeneity of norms and the fact that
\mathbb{C}(\delta)
is a cone. This concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 9

(
\ATP
)
.

Let
\mathsf{c}_{0},c_{0}\in(0,1)
be the universal constants of Proposition
8
. Given
\delta\in(0,1)
, let
\mathsf{h}_{\delta}
be the number in (
244
) and define the cone

\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\delta):=\left(\mathfrak{C}\left(\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{\delta}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\bigcap\mathds{C}_{\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}\left(\frac{4}{\mathsf{c}_{0}^{2}\sqrt{n}}\right).

Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
the design operator
\mathfrak{M}
satisfies

(254)

\displaystyle\inf_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\delta)}\frac{\|\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\|_{2}}{\|[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}}\geq\mathsf{c}_{0}/\sqrt{2}.

Proof.

The proof will happen on the event for which the statement of Proposition
8
is satisfied. First, we claim that, for all
[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathds{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(255)

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}.

Indeed,

\sum_{i\in[n]}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(n)},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\sharp}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle^{\sharp}\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp},

since
\min_{i\in[n]}\omega_{i}=\omega_{n}\geq 1
as the sequence
\{\omega_{i}\}
is non-increasing.

From the above fact and Proposition
8
, we have that, for all

[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\delta)
,

(256)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})+\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+2\langle\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle

(257)

\displaystyle\geq\mathsf{c}_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}-2\frac{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}

(258)

\displaystyle\geq(\mathsf{c}_{0}^{2}/2)\|\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\Pi}^{2}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

We now aim in proving
\MP
with
\mathcal{R}=\|\cdot\|_{N}
, the nuclear norm. We will use the following well known result which makes use of Bernstein-type inequalities for random matrices
[
3
]
,
[
4
]
.
7
7

7

Note that

\max_{k,\ell}\{R_{k},C_{\ell}\}\geq\frac{1}{d_{1}\wedge d_{2}}
.

Lemma 10.4

(Lemma 2 in
[
55
]
, Lemma 5 in
[
53
]
)
.

Suppose
\{(\eta,\mathbf{X})\}\cup\{(\eta_{i},\mathbf{X}_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
is an iid sequence taking values on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{X}
such that
\eta
has zero mean, variance
\sigma_{\eta}^{2}
and
0<|\eta|_{\psi_{2}}<\infty
. For
t>0
, define

(259)

\displaystyle\triangle_{\eta}(t):=\max\left\{\sigma_{\eta}\sqrt{\max_{k,\ell}\{R_{k},C_{\ell}\}\frac{(\log t+\log d)}{n}},|\eta|_{\psi_{2}}\log^{1/2}\left(\frac{|\eta|_{\psi_{2}}m}{\sigma_{\eta}}\right)\frac{(\log t+\log d)}{n}\right\}.

Then for some absolute constant
\mathsf{C}>0
, for all
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

(260)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\eta_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|_{\op}\leq\mathsf{C}\triangle_{\eta}(2/\delta).

Proposition 10

(
\MP
)
.

For all
\delta\in(0,1]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with probability of at least
1-\delta
, for all
[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathds{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(261)

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{p}\triangle_{\xi}(4/\delta)\cdot\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathbf{V})+C\sigma\frac{\sqrt{1+\log(2/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}+C\sigma\frac{\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{\sharp})}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}.

Proof.

From Lemma
10.4
, on an event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta/2
,

(262)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|_{\op}\leq\mathsf{C}\triangle_{\xi}(4/\delta).

We shall also use that, on an event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta/2
, for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(263)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(2/\delta)}{n}}+C\sigma\frac{\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{\sharp})}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}.

Indeed, from Lemma
8.13
in Section
8.3
with the set
U:=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{B}^{n}_{2}:\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}\leq r\}
we obtain that, with probability
\geq 1-\delta
,

(264)

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in U}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\sqrt{n}}\leq C\sigma\frac{\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{\sharp})}{\sqrt{n}}r+C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2\log(1/\delta)}{n}}.

This fact and Lemma
11.1
in the Appendix with set
V:=\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
and functions
M(\boldsymbol{u}):=-\langle\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle/\sqrt{n}
,
h(\boldsymbol{u}):=\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}
and
g(r):=r\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{\sharp})
entails that with probability
\geq 1-\delta
, for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
,

(265)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+C\sigma\frac{\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{\sharp})}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}.

This fact and homogeneity of norms imply that, on the same event, the property displayed above holds for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. This proves (
263
).

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta
, the inequality claimed in the lemma holds for all
[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathds{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
since, by the dual norm inequality,

(266)

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}

(267)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|_{\op}\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathbf{V})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}.

∎

10.1
Deterministic bounds

Throughout this section,
\mathcal{R}
is a any decomposable norm. For convenience, we will work with the normalized regularization
\mathcal{R}^{(p)}=\mathcal{R}/\sqrt{p}
. Recall Definition
8.6
, for given
c_{0},\gamma,\eta>0
, of the cone
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}(c_{0},\gamma,\eta)
.

Lemma 10.5
.

Suppose that
\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}
and

(i)

(\mathfrak{X},\boldsymbol{\xi})
satisfies

\MP_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)},\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{4})
in Definition
10.1
.

(ii)

\lambda=\gamma\tau\geq 2\mathsf{f}_{2},

\tau\geq 2\mathsf{f}_{4}.

Then

(268)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\triangle,

where

(269)

\displaystyle\triangle:=(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})(\mathcal{R}^{(p)}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})-(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})(\mathcal{R}^{(p)}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+(\nicefrac{{3\tau\Omega}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}-(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\sum_{i=o+1}^{n}\omega_{i}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})_{i}^{\sharp}.

In particular,
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}\left(3,\gamma,2\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}}{3\tau}+\Omega\right)
.

Proof sketch.

A similar argument used in Lemma
8.8
entails

(270)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\rangle+\lambda(\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathbf{B}^{*})-\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\hat{\mathbf{B}}))+\tau(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|_{\sharp}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_{\sharp}).

By condition (i)-(ii) and Lemmas
8.4
and
8.5
(with

\nu=1/2
), we obtain (
268
).
∎

Theorem 10.6

(Robust matrix completion)
.

Define the cone

(271)

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}:=\left(\mathfrak{C}\left(\mathsf{h}_{1}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{2}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\bigcap\mathds{C}_{\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{3}\right),

for positive constants
(\mathsf{h}_{1},\mathsf{h}_{2},\mathsf{h}_{3})
. Suppose that

(i)

(\mathfrak{X},\boldsymbol{\xi})
satisfies the

\MP_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)},\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{4})
.

(ii)

For some
\mathsf{c}_{1}>0
, the design satisfies

\inf_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathbb{C}}\frac{\|\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\|_{2}}{\|[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}}\geq\mathsf{c}_{1}.

(iii)

\lambda=\gamma\tau\geq 2\mathsf{f}_{2}

and
\tau\geq 2\mathsf{f}_{3}
.

Define the quantities

\mathcal{L}:=\frac{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{h}_{2}}{\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{h}_{3}}{\tau}

and

R:=\Psi_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})\right)\mu\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}(4)\right).

Then either
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\|_{\Pi}\leq 2\mathsf{a}\mathsf{h}_{1}
or

(272)

\displaystyle\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mathcal{L}\sqrt{64\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(32\tau\Omega+20\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}}\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{1}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\sqrt{2.25\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(6\tau\Omega+4\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}}\right],

(273)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq\left[8\mathcal{L}(4\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(8\tau\Omega+5\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2})\right]\bigvee\left[\frac{1}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}(9\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(16\tau\Omega+10\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2})\right].

Proof.

We will divide the proof in different cases. We recall that by Lemma
10.5
,
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathscr{C}
with
\mathscr{C}:=\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}(3,\gamma,\Omega+2\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}}{3\tau})
.

Case 1:

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\notin\mathfrak{C}\left(\mathsf{h}_{1}\right)
.

By definition,

\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\|_{\Pi}\leq\mathsf{h}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\mathsf{a}\mathsf{h}_{1}

and we are done.

Case 2:

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\notin\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{2}\right)
.

In that case

(274)

\displaystyle\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}\leq\mathsf{h}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\|_{\infty}\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})\leq 2\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{2}}}{{\lambda}})\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}).

We now consider two cases.

Case 2.1:

4\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))\geq\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))
.

We have
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}(4)
. Decomposability of
\mathcal{R}
,

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathscr{C}

and Cauchy-Schwarz further imply

(275)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq 4\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))+(4\tau\Omega+2\mathsf{f}_{1})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}

(276)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{16\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(4\tau\Omega+2\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}}\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}.

The above display and (
274
) imply

(277)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}\leq 4\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{2}}}{{\lambda}})\sqrt{4\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(2\tau\Omega+\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}},

and

(278)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\mathbf{B}}})+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}\leq 8\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{2}}}{{\lambda}})[4\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(2\tau\Omega+\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}].

Case 2.2:

4\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))<\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))
.

As

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathscr{C}
,

(279)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))+\tau\sum_{i=o+1}^{n}\omega_{i}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})^{\sharp}_{i}\leq(3\tau\Omega+2\mathsf{f}_{1})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}.

This fact, decomposability of
\mathcal{R}
and Cauchy-Schwarz imply

(280)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\big\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq 4\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))+(4\tau\Omega+2\mathsf{f}_{1})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}

(281)

\displaystyle\leq(16\tau\Omega+10\mathsf{f}_{1})\,\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\big\|_{2}.

The above display and (
274
) imply

(282)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}\leq 4\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{2}}}{{\lambda}})(8\tau\Omega+5\mathsf{f}_{1}),

and

(283)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\big\|_{\sharp}\leq 8\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{2}}}{{\lambda}})(8\tau\Omega+5\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}.

Case 3:

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\notin\mathds{C}_{\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{3}\right)
.

In that case

(284)

\displaystyle\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}\leq\mathsf{h}_{3}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}\leq 2\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{3}}}{{\tau}})\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}.

As in Case 2, by dividing in the same two subcases and using the bounds (
276
) and (
281
) we get

(285)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{3}}}{{\tau}})\sqrt{4\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(2\tau\Omega+\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}},

(286)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq 8\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{3}}}{{\tau}})[4\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(2\tau\Omega+\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}].

or

(287)

\displaystyle\big\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\big\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{3}}}{{\tau}})(8\tau\Omega+5\mathsf{f}_{1}),

(288)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\big\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq 8\mathsf{a}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{h}_{3}}}{{\tau}})(8\tau\Omega+5\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}.

Case 4:

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathbb{C}
.

As above, we further split in two cases.

Case 4.1:

4\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))\geq\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))
.

Hence

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}(4)
. Decomposability of
\mathcal{R}
,

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathscr{C}
and Cauchy-Schwarz give

(289)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\triangle

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))+(\nicefrac{{3\tau\Omega}}{{2}}+\mathsf{f}_{1})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}

(290)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}})\left\{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+\left(\tau\Omega+\frac{2\mathsf{f}_{1}}{3}\right)^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}.

From the above display, (
268
) and condition (ii), we obtain

(291)

\displaystyle\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}\leq(\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}})\frac{\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(\tau\Omega+(\nicefrac{{2\mathsf{f}_{1}}}{{3}}))^{2}}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}.

Finally, (
276
) and the previous display imply

(292)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(\tau\Omega+(\nicefrac{{2\mathsf{f}_{1}}}{{3}}))^{2}}\sqrt{4\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(2\tau\Omega+\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}}

(293)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}[9\lambda^{2}R^{2}+(3\tau\Omega+2\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}].

Case 4.2:

4\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))<\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))
.

As

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathscr{C}
, relation (
279
) holds. This fact implies that

(294)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\triangle

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}}))+(\nicefrac{{3\tau\Omega}}{{2}}+\mathsf{f}_{1})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}\leq(6\tau\Omega+4\mathsf{f}_{1})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}.

The above display, (
268
) and
condition (ii) yield

(295)

\displaystyle\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}\leq\frac{6\tau\Omega+4\mathsf{f}_{1}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}.

Finally, (
281
) and the previous display imply

(296)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}^{*}})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\big\|_{\sharp}\leq\frac{(16\tau\Omega+10\mathsf{f}_{1})^{2}}{\mathsf{c}_{1}^{2}}.

To finish, we note that the bounds in the statement of the theorem is the maximum of all the bounds established in the above cases.
∎

10.2
Proof of Theorem
4.4

We now set
\mathcal{R}:=\|\cdot\|_{N}
. We have

\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})=\sqrt{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{X}\left(\mathbb{B}_{\|\cdot\|_{N}}\right)\right)\right]\leq\sqrt{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i\in[n]}g_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|_{\op}\right],

with
\{g_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
iid
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
independent of

\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. The assumptions of Lemma
10.4
apply to

\eta=\mathcal{N}(0,1)
so integration yields

(297)

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})}{n}\lesssim\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{p\max_{k,\ell}\{R_{k},C_{\ell}\}}{n}(\log d)},\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n}(\log m)^{1/2}(\log d)\right\}.

As before, Proposition E.2 in
[
7
]
implies that

\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}_{\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}^{n})\lesssim 1
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
. Let the numbers

\mathsf{h}_{3}\asymp\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},

and

(298)

\displaystyle\mathsf{h}_{1}=\mathsf{h}_{2}

\displaystyle\asymp\sqrt{p\max_{k,\ell}\{R_{k},C_{\ell}\}\frac{\log d}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{p}\log^{1/2}(m)\frac{\log d}{n}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(1/\delta)}{n}},

and cone

(299)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\delta):=\mathfrak{C}\left(\mathsf{h}_{1}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\bigcap\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{R}^{(p)}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{2}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\bigcap\mathds{C}_{\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}\left(\mathsf{h}_{3}\right).

By Proposition
9
, there is universal constant
\mathsf{c}_{1}\in(0,1)
such that, on an event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta/2
,

(300)

\displaystyle\inf_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\delta)}\frac{\|\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\|_{2}}{\|[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}}\geq\mathsf{c}_{1}.

Take

(301)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{1}

\displaystyle\asymp\sigma\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(2/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}},

(302)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{2}

\displaystyle\asymp\max\left\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{p\max_{k,\ell}\{R_{k},C_{\ell}\}\frac{\log(4/\delta)+\log d}{n}},\sqrt{p}\sigma\log^{1/2}\left(\frac{\sigma m}{\sigma_{\xi}}\right)\frac{\log(4/\delta)+\log d}{n}\right\},

(303)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{4}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}.

By Proposition
10
, we have on an event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta/2
,
{\MP}_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{4})
is satisfied.

The proof will now hold on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta
. With the tuning parameters
\lambda\asymp(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{f}_{2}}}{{\sigma}})(\mathsf{a}\vee\sigma)
and
\tau\asymp(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{f}_{4}}}{{\sigma}})(\mathsf{a}\vee\sigma)
stated in Theorem
4.4
, all conditions of Theorem
10.6
hold on such event implying the claimed rates, noting that

R\leq\sqrt{r}\mu(\mathbf{B}^{*})

with

\mu(\mathbf{B}^{*}):=\mu^{(p)}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}^{(p)}}(4)\right)=\mu^{(p)}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(4)\right),

and that
\mathcal{L}\lesssim 1
, since
\mathsf{h}_{2}/\lambda\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{a}\vee\sigma}
and
\mathsf{h}_{3}/\tau\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{a}\vee\sigma}
.

11
Appendix

11.1
Peeling lemmas

Lemma 11.1

(
[
29
]
)
.

Let
g
be right-continuous, non-decreasing function
from
\mathbb{R}_{+}
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
h
be function from

V
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Assume that for some constants
b\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
c\geq 1
, for every
r>0
and for any
\delta\in(0,1/(c\vee 7))
, we have

(304)

\displaystyle A(r,\delta)=\Big\{\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq r,}M(\boldsymbol{v})\geq-g(r)-b\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\Big\},

with probability at least
1-c\delta
.

Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1/(c\vee 7))
, with probability at least
1-c\delta
, we have for all
\boldsymbol{v}\in V
,

(305)

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})\geq-1.2(g\circ h)(\boldsymbol{v})-b\big(3+\sqrt{\log(9/\delta)}\big).

Lemma 11.2

(
[
29
]
)
.

Let
g,\bar{g}
be right-continuous, non-decreasing functions
from
\mathbb{R}_{+}
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
h,\bar{h}
be functions from

V
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Assume that for some constants
b\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
c\geq 1
, for every
r,\bar{r}>0
and for any
\delta\in(0,1/(c\vee 7))
, we have

(306)

\displaystyle A(r,\bar{r},\delta)=\Big\{\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:(h,\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})\leq(r,\bar{r})}M(\boldsymbol{v})\geq-g(r)-\bar{g}(\bar{r})-b\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\Big\},

with probability at least
1-c\delta
.

Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1/(c\vee 7))
, with probability at least
1-c\delta
, we have for all
\boldsymbol{v}\in V
,

(307)

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})\geq-1.2(g\circ h)(\boldsymbol{v})-1.2(\bar{g}\circ\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})-b\big(4.8+\sqrt{\log(81/\delta)}\big).

Lemma 11.3
.

Let
g,\bar{g}
be right-continuous, non-decreasing functions
from
\mathbb{R}_{+}
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
h,\bar{h}
be functions from
V
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
b>0
be a constant and
c,c_{0}\geq 2
be universal constants. Assume that for every
r,\bar{r}>0
and every
\delta\in(0,1/c)
and every
\rho\in(0,1/c_{0})
, the event
A(r,\bar{r},\delta,\rho)
defined by the inequality

(308)

\displaystyle\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:(h,\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})\leq(r,\bar{r})}M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-\left[1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log(1/\rho)}\right]b\frac{g(r)}{\sqrt{n}}-b\frac{\bar{g}(\bar{r})}{\sqrt{n}}

(309)

\displaystyle-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}-\frac{b}{n}\log(1/\delta)-\frac{b}{n}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\log(1/\rho)},

has probability at least
1-c\delta-c_{0}\rho
.

Then, for universal constant
C>0
, with probability at least
1-\delta-\rho
, we have that for all
\boldsymbol{v}\in V
,

(310)

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-C\left[1+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\rho)}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]b\frac{(g\circ h)(\boldsymbol{v})}{\sqrt{n}}-Cb\frac{(\bar{g}\circ\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})}{\sqrt{n}}

(311)

\displaystyle-C\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}-C\frac{b}{n}\log(1/\delta)-C\frac{b}{n}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\log(1/\rho)}

(312)

\displaystyle-C\frac{b}{n}\sqrt{\log(1/\rho)}-C\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}[1+\sqrt{\log(1/\rho)}].

Proof.

The proof is an adaptation of the proof of Lemma
11.2
valid for subgaussian tails. The computations are slightly more involved in our case as

\sqrt{\log(1/\rho)}
multiplies the peeled function
g
. We only sketch the proof for the one-dimensional case (
\bar{g}\equiv 0
,
\bar{h}\equiv 0
,

\bar{r}\equiv 0
). During the proof,
C
is an universal constant that may change.

Let
\eta,\epsilon>1
be two parameters to be chosen later on. We set
\mu_{0}=0
and, for
k\geq 1
,
